FILE: /tests/scanner_tests.cpp
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
// Unit tests for the barcode decoder (pure C++, no camera): pictures are drawn in memory.
#include "decoder.h"

#include <cstdio>
#include <cstdlib>
#include <string>

static int failures = 0;
#define CHECK(c) do { if (!(c)) { std::printf("FAILED line %d: %s\n", __LINE__, #c); ++failures; } } while (0)

static std::string modulesFor(const std::string &d)
{
    std::string m = "101";
    for (int i = 0; i < 4; ++i) m += Decoder::patterns[d[i] - '0'];
    m += "01010";
    for (int i = 4; i < 8; ++i)
        for (const char *p = Decoder::patterns[d[i] - '0']; *p; ++p) m += (*p == '1') ? '0' : '1';
    return m + "101";
}

// Draw the barcode `scale` pixels per module, with quiet zones, inside a larger picture.
static GrayImage draw(const std::string &digits, int scale, int margin, bool upsideDown = false, bool sideways = false, int noise = 0)
{
    std::string m = modulesFor(digits);
    if (upsideDown) m.assign(m.rbegin(), m.rend());
    const int w = static_cast<int>(m.size()) * scale + 2 * margin, h = 120;
    GrayImage g;
    g.width = sideways ? h : w;
    g.height = sideways ? w : h;
    g.pixels.assign(static_cast<std::size_t>(g.width) * g.height, 235);
    std::srand(7);
    for (int x = 0; x < w; ++x)
    {
        const int mi = (x - margin) / scale;
        const bool dark = x >= margin && mi >= 0 && mi < static_cast<int>(m.size()) && m[static_cast<std::size_t>(mi)] == '1';
        for (int y = 20; y < 100; ++y)
        {
            int v = dark ? 20 : 235;
            if (noise) v += std::rand() % (2 * noise + 1) - noise;
            v = v < 0 ? 0 : (v > 255 ? 255 : v);
            const int px = sideways ? y : x, py = sideways ? x : y;
            g.pixels[static_cast<std::size_t>(py) * g.width + px] = static_cast<std::uint8_t>(v);
        }
    }
    return g;
}

int main()
{
    Decoder d;
    std::string out;
    CHECK(Decoder::hasValidCheckDigit("40001029"));
    CHECK(!Decoder::hasValidCheckDigit("40001028"));

    for (const char *code : {"40001029", "40002149", "40003511", "96385074", "00000000"})
    {
        if (!Decoder::hasValidCheckDigit(code)) continue;
        CHECK(d.decodeCropping(draw(code, 3, 20), out) && out == code);
        CHECK(d.decodeCropping(draw(code, 2, 30), out) && out == code);
        CHECK(d.decodeCropping(draw(code, 5, 10), out) && out == code);
        CHECK(d.decodeCropping(draw(code, 3, 20, true), out) && out == code);        // upside down
        CHECK(d.decodeCropping(draw(code, 3, 20, false, true), out) && out == code); // sideways
        CHECK(d.decodeCropping(draw(code, 4, 20, false, false, 18), out) && out == code); // camera noise
    }
    // barcode only in the lower part of the picture: found when the aiming line is moved onto it
    {
        GrayImage g = draw("40001029", 3, 20);
        for (int y = 0; y < 60; ++y)                       // blank out the top half of the bars
            for (int x = 0; x < g.width; ++x) g.pixels[static_cast<std::size_t>(y) * g.width + x] = 235;
        CHECK(d.decodeCropping(g, out, 0.8) && out == "40001029");
    }
    // wrong check digit: not accepted, but reported
    CHECK(!d.decodeCropping(draw("40001028", 3, 20), out));
    CHECK(d.isBadCheck());
    // blank picture
    GrayImage blank; blank.width = 200; blank.height = 100; blank.pixels.assign(20000, 200);
    CHECK(!d.decodeCropping(blank, out));
    CHECK(!d.decodeCropping(GrayImage{}, out));

    if (failures) { std::printf("%d scanner test(s) FAILED\n", failures); return 1; }
    std::printf("scanner tests OK\n");
    return 0;
}

# Changelog

All notable changes to **BarcodeHub** are documented in this file.

## [3.0.0] — Increment 3: Inventory, Discounts, Reporting & Advanced Billing

### Planned

- Add product category management through the GUI, stored in `PCategory.txt`.
- Use the first two digits of every barcode as the product category.
- Store the category ID of each product in `Product.txt`.
- Allow managers to generate barcodes and to add or edit products.
- Add an inventory view and inventory updates for managers.
- Update available product quantities with every order.
- Allow existing orders to be opened and edited; paid orders only within 7 days of payment and after manager approval.
- Reduce the clicks needed to record an order, put an order on hold, and resume an on-hold order.
- Extend `Bill.txt` with amount received, balance returned, seasonal discount, special discount, bill date, and bill time.
- Introduce `BillDetail.txt` to store the product lines of each bill.
- Introduce `Seasons.txt` for seasonal discounts, and allow managers to add discounts and set them active or inactive.
- Apply the seasonal discount automatically when the bill date falls within an active season.
- Support special discounts of up to 8 percent.
- Print bills as PDF for cashiers and managers.
- Add management reports: incomplete orders, completed orders, cash sales, all sales, top ten most sold items, and top ten items by remaining stock.
- Provide separate cashier and manager features in the user interface.

### UI progress (front end only, back end unchanged)

- Orders page: "Editable for N days" badge on paid orders (7-day window, amber for the last 3 days, "Window closed" afterwards), **Open** for paid orders (manager approval for cashiers) and **Resume** for in-progress and on-hold orders, including orders parked with F2.
- Record Order: opening an existing order loads it into the cart ("editing" mode). Saving edits to an existing order is not wired yet because it needs the Increment 3 back end.
- Reports page: six tabs (incomplete orders, completed orders, cash sales with daily/weekly/monthly/yearly/custom range, all sales, top 10 sold, top 10 by remaining stock) with summary tiles, totals, CSV export and PDF print.

### Development Notes

- Increment 3 builds on the Point of Sale system from Increment 2.
- Requirements and open design decisions are documented in `Project-Planning/Increment-3.md`.
- Increment 3 is currently in the planning stage. None of the items listed above are implemented yet.

## [2.0.0] — Increment 2: Point of Sale System

### Planned

- Introduce a GUI-based Point of Sale (POS) workflow.
- Implement product management using `Product.txt`.
- Add product lookup by barcode and product ID.
- Display product specifications and validate available stock.
- Integrate camera-based barcode scanning with the POS.
- Implement cart and order management.
- Generate unique 8-digit Order IDs.
- Store order records in `Order.txt`.
- Store product details and quantities in `OrderDetail.txt`.
- Implement bill generation and payment calculations.
- Save generated bills in `Bill.txt`.
- Support payment modes: cash, card, and wallet.

### Development Notes

- Increment 2 extends the existing barcode generator and reader from Increment 1.
- The system is being developed using modular components to promote separation of responsibilities, reusability, and maintainability.
- Increment 2 is currently in development.

## [1.0.0] — Increment 1: Initial Project Setup

### Added

- Established the initial project structure and directory organization.
- Configured CMake as the project build system.
- Implemented the Qt Widgets-based desktop application foundation.
- Created the initial application window and navigation.
- Implemented barcode generation for six-digit numeric input.
- Implemented barcode reading functionality.
- Added the Decode Barcode interface with camera and image-upload sections.
- Introduced the initial Scan Logs interface.
- Organized backend modules for barcode generation, reading, and scanning.
- Added project documentation and contribution guidelines.

### Notes

- Established the foundational barcode generation and reading functionality.
- Increment 1 serves as the base for the POS capabilities introduced in Increment 2.

cmake_minimum_required(VERSION 3.21)

# Windows + Ninja: Qt Creator sometimes hands CMake Visual Studio's cl.exe together with a MinGW
# Qt kit, which cannot compile. Unless an MSVC Qt is being used, switch to Qt's own MinGW g++.
if(WIN32 AND NOT CMAKE_GENERATOR MATCHES "Visual Studio"
   AND (NOT CMAKE_CXX_COMPILER OR CMAKE_CXX_COMPILER MATCHES "[cC][lL]\\.exe$")
   AND NOT "${CMAKE_PREFIX_PATH}${Qt6_DIR}${QT_DIR}" MATCHES "[mM][sS][vV][cC]")
    include("${CMAKE_CURRENT_LIST_DIR}/cmake/toolchain-qt-mingw.cmake")
endif()

project(BarcodePOS LANGUAGES CXX)

set(CMAKE_CXX_STANDARD 17)
set(CMAKE_CXX_STANDARD_REQUIRED ON)

if(MSVC)
    # Qt 6 needs a correct __cplusplus value; also read sources as UTF-8.
    add_compile_options(/Z-c:__cplusplus /utf-8 /permissive-)
endif()

option(BACKEND_ONLY "Build backend and tests without Qt" OFF)

# Camera support uses Qt Multimedia (shipped with Qt; no OpenCV needed):
#   AUTO (default) - camera if the Qt Multimedia component is installed, otherwise built without it
#   ON             - camera required: configuring fails if Qt Multimedia is missing
#   OFF            - no camera (typed barcode lookup only)
set(CAMERA_MODE AUTO CACHE STRING "Camera: AUTO, ON, OFF")
set_property(CACHE CAMERA_MODE PROPERTY STRINGS AUTO ON OFF)
# Which library grabs the pictures (the barcode decoder itself is always the same pure C++ code):
#   AUTO   - OpenCV if a build matching the compiler is found, otherwise Qt Multimedia
#   QT     - Qt Multimedia only
#   OPENCV - OpenCV only (set OpenCV_DIR; it must be built for the SAME compiler as Qt:
#            the official OpenCV Windows download is MSVC-only -> use it with the MSVC kit)
set(CAMERA_BACKEND AUTO CACHE STRING "Camera library: AUTO, QT, OPENCV")
set_property(CACHE CAMERA_BACKEND PROPERTY STRINGS AUTO QT OPENCV)

# ============================================================
# QT CONFIGURATION

set(QT_INSTALL_ROOT "C:/Qt"
    CACHE PATH "Qt installation root"
)

if(WIN32 AND NOT CMAKE_PREFIX_PATH AND NOT Qt6_DIR)

    file(GLOB _qt_roots
        "${QT_INSTALL_ROOT}/6.*/mingw_64"
    )

    if(_qt_roots)
        list(SORT _qt_roots
            COMPARE NATURAL
            ORDER DESCENDING
        )

        list(GET _qt_roots 0 _qt_root)
        list(PREPEND CMAKE_PREFIX_PATH "${_qt_root}")
    endif()

endif()

set(CMAKE_AUTOMOC ON)
set(CMAKE_AUTOUIC ON)
set(CMAKE_AUTORCC ON)

find_package(QT NAMES Qt6 Qt5
    REQUIRED COMPONENTS Widgets
)

find_package(Qt${QT_VERSION_MAJOR}
    REQUIRED COMPONENTS Widgets
)

# A MinGW build of Qt cannot be compiled with MSVC (this is what "Qt requires a C++17 compiler ...
# /Zc:__cplusplus" means: the kit picked cl.exe). Stop early with the fix instead of 100 errors.
if(MSVC AND "${Qt${QT_VERSION_MAJOR}_DIR}" MATCHES "mingw")
    message(FATAL_ERROR
        "Qt is a MinGW build (${Qt${QT_VERSION_MAJOR}_DIR}) but the compiler is MSVC (${CMAKE_CXX_COMPILER}).\n"
        "Fix: Qt Creator > Projects > Build & Run > use the kit 'Desktop Qt 6.x.x MinGW 64-bit' "
        "(its compiler must be 'MinGW ... 64-bit', not 'Microsoft Visual C++'), delete the build folder, "
        "and configure again. Or use the 'Desktop Qt 6.x.x MSVC2022 64-bit' kit instead.")
endif()

set(CAMERA_ENABLED OFF)
set(CAMERA_LIB "")
if(NOT CAMERA_MODE STREQUAL "OFF")
    if(NOT CAMERA_BACKEND STREQUAL "QT")
        # MinGW: first look for an OpenCV config built for MinGW (x64/mingw/lib), then for loose
        # headers + libopencv_*.dll files. (The official Windows download is MSVC-only.)
        if(MINGW)
            file(GLOB_RECURSE _mingw_cfg "$ENV{USERPROFILE}/Downloads/opencv/*mingw*/OpenCVConfig.cmake"
                                         "${OpenCV_DIR}/*mingw*/OpenCVConfig.cmake" "C:/opencv/*mingw*/OpenCVConfig.cmake")
            if(_mingw_cfg AND NOT OpenCV_DIR)
                list(GET _mingw_cfg 0 _first_cfg)
                get_filename_component(_cfg_dir "${_first_cfg}" DIRECTORY)
                set(OpenCV_DIR "${_cfg_dir}" CACHE PATH "OpenCV (MinGW)" FORCE)
            endif()
        endif()
        find_package(OpenCV 4 QUIET COMPONENTS core imgproc videoio)
        if(OpenCV_FOUND)
            set(CAMERA_ENABLED ON)
            set(CAMERA_LIB OPENCV)
            message(STATUS "Camera: OpenCV ${OpenCV_VERSION} (${OpenCV_DIR})")
        elseif(MINGW)
            list(APPEND CMAKE_MODULE_PATH "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/cmake")
            include(FindMinGWOpenCV)
            if(MINGW_OPENCV_FOUND)
                set(OpenCV_INCLUDE_DIRS "${MINGW_OPENCV_INCLUDE}")
                set(OpenCV_LIBS ${MINGW_OPENCV_LIBS})
                set(OPENCV_DLL_DIR "${MINGW_OPENCV_DLL_DIR}")
                set(CAMERA_ENABLED ON)
                set(CAMERA_LIB OPENCV)
                message(STATUS "Camera: OpenCV (MinGW files found without a CMake config)")
            endif()
        endif()
    endif()
    if(NOT CAMERA_ENABLED AND NOT CAMERA_BACKEND STREQUAL "OPENCV" AND QT_VERSION_MAJOR EQUAL 6)
        find_package(Qt6 QUIET COMPONENTS Multimedia)
        if(TARGET Qt6::Multimedia)
            set(CAMERA_ENABLED ON)
            set(CAMERA_LIB QT)
            message(STATUS "Camera: Qt Multimedia ${Qt6Multimedia_VERSION}")
        endif()
    endif()
    if(NOT CAMERA_ENABLED)
        set(_why "No usable camera library: install Qt Multimedia (Qt Maintenance Tool > Add or remove components > "
                 "Additional Libraries > Qt Multimedia), or give OpenCV_DIR an OpenCV built for THIS compiler "
                 "(the official OpenCV Windows download only works with the MSVC kit, not MinGW).")
        if(CAMERA_MODE STREQUAL "ON")
            message(FATAL_ERROR ${_why})
        else()
            message(STATUS "Camera disabled. ${_why}")
        endif()
    endif()
endif()

# ============================================================

# Every page / dialog lives in its own file under Frontend/src/ui. New files are picked up automatically
# (re-run CMake). main.cpp, CameraWidget.cpp and POSWindow.cpp there are legacy stand-alone files.
file(GLOB UI_SOURCES CONFIGURE_DEPENDS
    Frontend/src/ui/*.cpp
)

# Legacy stand-alone files are skipped. [Mm]ain also matches the old Main.cpp test window,
# which includes CameraWidget.h (needs OpenCV) and has its own main().
list(FILTER UI_SOURCES EXCLUDE REGEX
    "/([Mm]ain|CameraWidget|POSWindow)\\.cpp$"
)

    Frontend/include/ui/*.h
)

list(FILTER UI_HEADERS EXCLUDE REGEX
    "CameraWidget.h|POSWindow.h"
)

    ${UI_SOURCES}
    ${UI_HEADERS}

    Backend/include/product

        target_link_libraries(barcode_ui PUBLIC Qt6::Multimedia)
        target_compile_definitions(barcode_ui PUBLIC WITH_CAMERA_QT)
    else()
        target_include_directories(barcode_ui PUBLIC ${OpenCV_INCLUDE_DIRS})
        target_link_libraries(barcode_ui PUBLIC ${OpenCV_LIBS})
        target_compile_definitions(barcode_ui PUBLIC WITH_CAMERA_OPENCV)
    endif()

# OpenCV camera build on Windows: copy the OpenCV runtime DLLs next to BarcodeHub.exe.
if(WIN32 AND CAMERA_LIB STREQUAL "OPENCV")
    file(GLOB _opencv_dlls
        "${OpenCV_DIR}/x64/vc16/bin/*.dll" "${OpenCV_DIR}/x64/vc17/bin/*.dll"
        "${OpenCV_DIR}/../bin/*.dll" "${OpenCV_DIR}/../../bin/*.dll"
        "${OPENCV_DLL_DIR}/*.dll" "${OPENCV_DLL_DIR}/../bin/*.dll")
    if(_opencv_dlls)
        add_custom_command(TARGET BarcodeHub POST_BUILD
            COMMAND ${CMAKE_COMMAND} -E copy_if_different ${_opencv_dlls} "$<TARGET_FILE_DIR:BarcodeHub>"
            VERBATIM COMMENT "Copying OpenCV runtime DLLs")
    else()
        message(WARNING "No OpenCV DLLs found near ${OpenCV_DIR}; add the OpenCV bin folder to PATH to run the exe.")
    endif()
endif()

# Preserve existing records during rebuilding.
# Only copy starter data files when they do not already exist.

add_custom_command(TARGET BarcodeHub POST_BUILD
    COMMAND ${CMAKE_COMMAND}
        "-DSOURCE_DATA=${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/data"
        "-DDEST_DATA=$<TARGET_FILE_DIR:BarcodeHub>/data"
        -P "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/cmake/seed-data.cmake"
    VERBATIM
)

# ============================================================
# WINDOWS DEPLOYMENT (OPTIONAL)
# ============================================================

# Deployment is not part of the normal build. Qt Creator sets up the
# Qt/MinGW environment when launching an application using its Qt kit.
# To copy Qt DLLs and plugins for standalone distribution, build the
# BarcodeHub_deploy target explicitly instead.

if(WIN32)
    # Prefer the deployment tool belonging to the Qt version used by this kit.
    if(TARGET Qt${QT_VERSION_MAJOR}::windeployqt)
        get_target_property(WINDEPLOYQT_EXECUTABLE
            Qt${QT_VERSION_MAJOR}::windeployqt IMPORTED_LOCATION)
    endif()

    if(NOT WINDEPLOYQT_EXECUTABLE)
        find_program(WINDEPLOYQT_EXECUTABLE
            NAMES windeployqt
            HINTS
                "${Qt${QT_VERSION_MAJOR}_DIR}/../../../bin"
                "${QT_INSTALL_ROOT}/6.11.0/mingw_64/bin"
        )
    endif()

    if(WINDEPLOYQT_EXECUTABLE)
        set(_deploy_apps BarcodeHub)
        if(TARGET CameraDemo)
            list(APPEND _deploy_apps CameraDemo)
        endif()

        foreach(app IN LISTS _deploy_apps)
            add_custom_target(${app}_deploy
                COMMAND "${WINDEPLOYQT_EXECUTABLE}"
                    --no-translations
                    "$<TARGET_FILE:${app}>"
                DEPENDS ${app}
                VERBATIM
                COMMENT "Deploying Qt runtime for ${app}"
            )
        endforeach()
    else()
        message(STATUS
            "windeployqt not found; optional deployment targets disabled")
    endif()
endif()

FILE: /README.md
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
<h1 align="center">🏷️ BarcodeHub</h1>

<p align="center">
  <b>A C++17-based Point of Sale system with barcode scanning, product management, order processing, and billing.</b>
  <br />
  Developed as an academic project for Introduction to Software Engineering at FAST-NUCES.
</p>

<p align="center">
  <img src="https://img.shields.io/badge/C%2B%2B-17-00599C?style=for-the-badge&logo=cplusplus&logoColor=white" />
  <img src="https://img.shields.io/badge/Qt-6-41CD52?style=for-the-badge&logo=qt&logoColor=white" />
  <img src="https://img.shields.io/badge/OpenCV-5C3EE8?style=for-the-badge&logo=opencv&logoColor=white" />
  <img src="https://img.shields.io/badge/CMake-Build%20System-064F8C?style=for-the-badge&logo=cmake&logoColor=white" />
  <img src="https://img.shields.io/badge/Platform-Windows%20x64-0078D4?style=for-the-badge&logo=windows&logoColor=white" />
</p>

---

## 📌 Project Overview

**BarcodeHub** is a C++ desktop application being developed as a barcode-based Point of Sale (POS) system.

The project extends its initial barcode generation and reading capabilities into a GUI-based retail workflow, allowing cashiers to scan products, retrieve product information, manage orders, and generate bills. Later increments add category-based barcodes, inventory management, discounts, and management reports.

Developed incrementally, BarcodeHub emphasizes modularity, code quality, and reusability.

---

## ✨ Key Features

### v1.0.0

- 🏷️ Barcode generation for supported six-digit numeric product codes.
- 📖 Barcode reading functionality.
- 🖥️ Qt Widgets-based desktop application foundation.

### v2.0.0

- 📷 Camera-based barcode scanning.
- 📦 Product lookup and description from `Product.txt`.
- 🛒 Cart and order management.
- 📊 Product stock validation.
- 🧾 Automated bill generation.
- 💳 Cash, card, and wallet payment modes.
- 📁 File-based product, order, and billing records.
- 🎨 GUI-based Point of Sale workflow.

### v3.0.0

- 🗂️ Product category management, with the first two barcode digits representing the category.
- ➕ Manager tools to generate barcodes and to add or edit products.
- 📦 Inventory view and inventory updates, with stock updated on every order.
- ✏️ Opening and editing existing orders (paid orders only within 7 days of payment and after manager approval).
- ⚡ Fast order recording, with easy on-hold and resume.
- 🧾 Extended bills with bill details, amount received, and balance returned.
- 🌦️ Seasonal discounts from `Seasons.txt`, plus special discounts of up to 8 percent.
- 🖨️ Bill printing as PDF.
- 📈 Management reports: incomplete orders, completed orders, cash sales, all sales, top ten most sold items, and top ten items by remaining stock.
- 👥 Separate cashier and manager features.

---

## 🔄 POS Workflow

The POS workflow consists of four main modules:

1. **Product Management** — Retrieve product information and validate stock.
2. **Camera & Scanner** — Scan product barcodes using a connected camera.
3. **Order Management** — Record orders and manage product quantities.
4. **Billing** — Calculate totals, generate bills, and record payment information.

---

## 📄 Data Management

BarcodeHub uses text files to store its records:

| File | Purpose | Increment |
|---|---|---|
| `Product.txt` | Product specifications and available stock | 2 (category ID added in 3) |
| `Order.txt` | Customer and order information | 2 |
| `OrderDetail.txt` | Products and quantities associated with orders | 2 |
| `Bill.txt` | Generated bill information and payment status | 2 (extended in 3) |
| `PCategory.txt` | Product categories (ID, name, acronym) | 3 (planned) |
| `BillDetail.txt` | Product lines belonging to each bill | 3 (planned) |
| `Seasons.txt` | Seasonal discount periods and their status | 3 (planned) |

Each record follows the specified field order and uses `#` as its end-of-record marker.

---

## 🛠️ Technology Stack

| Technology | Purpose |
|---|---|
| **C++17** | Core application logic |
| **Qt 6 Widgets** | Desktop graphical interface |
| **OpenCV** | Camera capture and image processing |
| **CMake** | Build configuration |
| **MinGW-w64** | Windows C++ compilation |
| **PowerShell** | Build automation |
| **Git & GitHub** | Version control and collaboration |
| **GitHub Actions** | Automated branch synchronization |
